an ink footer (wordmark, address, both real contacts, The Home / Plan columns incl. the real Vrbo listing, dynamic year, <strong>&ldquo;Concept by Bofill Technologies&rdquo;</strong> credit). <strong>Motion (rolled: parallax)</strong>: CSS <code>background-attachment: fixed</code> on the two photo bands at &ge;1200px only, plus light IntersectionObserver reveals; <code>prefers-reduced-motion</code> kills both. <strong>Built strictly to the Web Architect SOP</strong>: flat <code>index.html</code> + <code>/css/style.css</code> + <code>/js/main.js</code> + <code>/img/</code> (manifest), <strong>zero inline <code>style=&quot;&quot;</code>, zero &lt;style&gt; blocks, zero executable inline &lt;script&gt; (JSON-LD + external src defer only), zero onclick handlers, zero JS <code>.style</code> assignments</strong>, BEM throughout, CSS custom properties at <code>:root</code>, mobile-first (560 / 640 / 900 breakpoints), vanilla ES6+ IIFE, WordPress-canonical nav classes. Semantic HTML5, WCAG 2.1 (skip link, exactly one h1, alt on every image, explicit width/height, lazy below the fold, a titled map iframe, focus-visible, a <code>no-js</code> class so every reveal shows with JS disabled, a <code>[hidden]</code> hard override so the logo/wordmark swap can never double-render), <strong>VacationRental JSON-LD</strong> (PostalAddress + telephone + email + petsAllowed + numberOfBedrooms 2 + 2.5 baths + occupancy 6 + floorSize 1200 + 8 amenityFeature + sameAs Vrbo), full OG / Twitter / geo meta, canonical, <code>prefers-reduced-motion</code> honored. QA&rsquo;d via Puppeteer at <strong>1440 + 390</strong> &mdash; <strong>0px horizontal overflow at both widths</strong>, exactly one h1, <strong>21 of 21 reveals fire</strong>, 14 images all with alt (12 lazy), 0 inline style attributes, 0 onclick handlers, 0 JS errors, the rail persists at both widths (full links at 1440, burger + phone circle + Book at 390), the bottom-sheet drawer opens / Escape-closes / scroll-locks. One real bug was found and fixed in QA: the CSS reset&rsquo;s <code>img { display:block }</code> was overriding the UA&rsquo;s <code>[hidden]</code> rule, double-rendering the logo and its wordmark fallback &mdash; fixed with an explicit <code>[hidden] { display:none !important }</code>. <strong>Images</strong>: 14 real property photos + the real logo <strong>hotlinked from the client&rsquo;s own WordPress uploads</strong> (2015 shoot at full resolution, <code>-200x200</code> gallery thumbs traced back to their source files); <code>/img/README.md</code> documents every slot for re-hosting. <strong>Caveats</strong>: the sandbox is firewalled from northharbormontauk.com, so photos and the map render blank in build QA (fine live / on GitHub Pages) and photo orientations could not be verified &mdash; every slot uses an aspect-ratio frame with <code>object-fit: cover</code> so any orientation reads correctly; swap crops if a frame cuts awkwardly. The email was recovered by decoding the site&rsquo;s Cloudflare obfuscation. The photography is the 2015 shoot &mdash; a current re-shoot (especially one frame of a yacht actually passing the deck, which the whole page is titled on) is the single biggest upgrade available. The site&rsquo;s &ldquo;Bosh&rdquo; spelling was corrected to Bosch. BookifyPro is Bofill&rsquo;s own engine &mdash; wire date params into the rail if the booking page accepts them. Homepage-only delivery. <strong>Rev. 2 (2026-08-16, per Steve): &ldquo;use logo NHlogo.png and give me nice header/navigation.&rdquo;</strong> The persistent bottom rail was replaced with a <strong>standard sticky top header</strong> &mdash; the <strong>real NHlogo.png</strong> hotlinked left (JS wordmark fallback, ellipsis-safe at 390px), six centred links with an oak hover underline, tap-to-call + a lake-blue Book Now right, a shadow gained on scroll; below 1024px the links move into a full-screen ink drawer (aria-expanded, Escape-close, scroll-lock) and <strong>the phone circle + Book Now stay in the bar at 390px</strong>. One real bug found and fixed in this pass: the wordmark fallback overflowed 105px at 390px &mdash; fixed with a clamped font size, min-width:0 and ellipsis on the brand. Re-QA&rsquo;d at 1440 + 390: 0px overflow both widths, 21/21 reveals, drawer verified, 0 JS errors. Nav pattern in the ledger is now effectively <strong>top-sticky</strong>. | Marcellus + Manrope | 2026-08-16 |

a photographic <strong>final CTA</strong> (&ldquo;Come get warm.&rdquo;) &rarr; a spruce-ink footer (real logo, real Facebook and Tripadvisor, Stay / Visit columns, both real mailboxes, dynamic-year copyright, <strong>&ldquo;Concept by Bofill Technologies&rdquo;</strong> credit). <strong>Booking bar (the fixed mechanic)</strong>: a slim spruce bar with the ember rule as its top border, revealing once the hero scrolls out &mdash; one king, four queens, one twin &mdash; 3 full baths, sleeps 12, newly renovated kitchen and baths, A/C, expansive porch and widow&rsquo;s walk, steps from Crescent Beach), the real 2026/27 rates (<strong>$7,500 and $12,500 per week high season; It runs as the hero&rsquo;s measure (<code>The mainland &#9500;&mdash;&mdash; 13 miles &mdash;&mdash;&#9508; Block Island</code>, collapsing to just the distance on phones), as the miniature glyph in front of every eyebrow, as the rule under the rate table, and as the pets-policy footnote line. Hand-built in CSS (<code>repeating-linear-gradient</code> ticks over a hairline) &mdash; no images, no icon font. <strong>SECOND DEVICE: THE DIPTYCH.</strong> Because there are exactly two houses, the hero is a two-plate collage &mdash; the MacGregor and the Beach House side by side, offset in height, each stamped with its own name tag, with a porch frame dropped across the seam. It stacks to two generous full-width plates on a phone. <strong>THIRD DEVICE: THE 1907 NUMERAL</strong> &mdash; the founding year set huge and outlined in beach-rose stroke; earned, because &ldquo;continuously rented every summer since 1907&rdquo; is the single most remarkable fact about the property. <strong>Palette sampled from the property&rsquo;s own world, not a generic coastal blue</strong>: <strong>salt-pond green-black #1C2B2A / #142120</strong> (the rail, the copy panels, the footer, the booking bar &mdash; a green-black taken from the pond at dusk, deliberately not navy and not near-black) + <strong>beach-rose #C04A63 / #A2384E</strong> (rosa rugosa, which covers Block Island &mdash; every CTA, every accent, the rule marks; a deliberate, unmistakably-not-terracotta accent) + <strong>weathered shingle #7E8A85</strong> (cedar gone gray-green &mdash; meta text, hairlines) + <strong>chalk #F6F6F2 / #FBFBF8</strong> paper + <strong>sand #E4DED1</strong> borders + <strong>mist #DFE6E3</strong> cool pale bands. Clears all three AI defaults &mdash; the ground is a cool chalk rather than warm cream, the accent is a rose pink rather than terracotta, and the dark is a pond green rather than near-black. <strong>Type (both fresh to the ledger)</strong>: <strong>Yeseva One</strong> (an art-nouveau-flavoured display serif &mdash; period-true for an 1900s Mansard-roofed Victorian, used large and sparingly for the hero, section titles, house names, the numeral and every stat figure) + <strong>Space Grotesk</strong> (a technical grotesque carrying body, nav, labels and the tracked-caps eyebrows &mdash; the deliberate tension of a Victorian display against a chart-like utility face, matching &ldquo;1907 house / thirteen nautical miles&rdquo;). Avoids the Fraunces / Work Sans / Bodoni Moda / Epilogue / Syne / Petrona / Gloock / Familjen Grotesk / Big Shoulders / Sora / DM Mono run of the last five builds. <strong>NAV (rolled: SIDE RAIL &mdash; deliberately not the top-sticky, top-sticky, centred-split-logo of the last three builds)</strong>: a fixed 118px chalk rail down the left edge at &ge;1180px carrying a <strong>vertical rotated wordmark</strong>, vertical rotated links (Houses / The Island / Weeks / Gallery / Visit), a tap-to-call circle and a vertical rose Reserve button at the foot; below 1180px it becomes a light top bar keeping the <strong>phone circle and Reserve visible at 390px</strong> with the links in a full-screen pond-green drawer (aria-expanded, Escape-close, scroll-lock, resize-close). The rail is deliberately light because the property has no logo. <strong>HERO (rolled: asymmetric collage)</strong> &mdash; the two-house diptych right, copy left, <strong>zero scrims or overlays on any photograph</strong> (per Steve&rsquo;s standing rule &mdash; no shade on any photo; the parallax bands put copy in solid pond panels instead), an H1 of their own idea (&ldquo;Two houses, <em>thirteen miles out.</em>&rdquo;), their real lede verbatim, dual CTA, the distance rule, and a four-cell stat row (Two houses / sleeping 9 &amp; 12 / rented by the week / in the family since 1907). <strong>Sections (rolled &mdash; leads with the two houses, not an about; the heritage story sits late, after both view bands; rates get a full section of their own because weekly rentals live or die on them)</strong>: <strong>Two houses. One island.</strong> (the two properties as alternating bento rows, each with a lead plate + a detail plate, a real 4-cell spec table, real bed-configuration chips and its own Enquire button that pre-fills that house) &rarr; a parallax <strong>&ldquo;Graceful porches, commanding views&rdquo;</strong> band &rarr; a parallax <strong>Crescent Beach</strong> band (the <em>Coastal Living</em> / <em>Elle</em> credential carried in copy, with no borrowed magazine artwork) &rarr; <strong>Since 1907</strong> (the outlined numeral + the real O&rsquo;Brien story + their own thank-you to repeat guests) &rarr; <strong>How a week works here</strong> (a real captioned rate <code>&lt;table&gt;</code> with row headers, plus six honest booking notes) &rarr; a bento <strong>gallery</strong> &rarr; <strong>Getting here</strong> (a <strong>real Google Maps embed</strong> at the property&rsquo;s own published coordinates 41.184112 / -71.5698413, the real PO Box, and the real ferry line) &rarr; a parallax <strong>final CTA</strong> &rarr; a pond-ink footer (real Facebook + Instagram, three columns, dynamic-year copyright, <strong>&ldquo;Concept by Bofill Technologies&rdquo;</strong> credit). <strong>Booking bar (the fixed mechanic, rebuilt for this property&rsquo;s real rule)</strong>: not check-in / check-out &mdash; a <strong>House / Arriving Sunday / Weeks</strong> selector, because they rent Sunday to Sunday. The date field <strong>defaults to the next actual Sunday</strong> and the number of weeks computes the departure date. Since the live site runs <strong>no booking engine</strong> (reservations are taken by email, phone and their own inquiry form), the bar and every Enquire button compose a <strong>pre-filled enquiry email</strong> carrying the chosen house, arrival, departure and length of stay &mdash; a marked hook in <code>js/main.js</code> is where a real engine gets wired in. <strong>Motion (rolled: parallax)</strong> &mdash; CSS <code>background-attachment: fixed</code> on the three photographic bands at &ge;1200px only (no JS scroll math), plus light IntersectionObserver reveals; <code>prefers-reduced-motion</code> kills both. <strong>Built strictly to the Web Architect SOP</strong>: flat <code>index.html</code> at root + <code>/css/style.css</code> + <code>/js/main.js</code> + <code>/img/</code> (manifest), <strong>zero inline <code>style=&quot;&quot;</code>, zero &lt;style&gt; blocks, zero executable inline &lt;script&gt; (JSON-LD + external src defer only), zero onclick handlers, zero JS <code>.style</code> assignments</strong> (all state via class toggles), BEM throughout, CSS custom properties at <code>:root</code>, mobile-first (560/780/1000/1180/1200/1340 breakpoints), vanilla ES6+ IIFE, WordPress-canonical nav classes (<code>.nav-primary</code>, <code>ul.menu.genesis-nav-menu.menu-primary</code>, <code>.menu-item</code>). Semantic HTML5, WCAG 2.1 (skip link, focus-visible, exactly one h1, alt on every image, explicit width/height, lazy below the fold, a captioned rate table with row headers, a titled map iframe, a <code>no-js</code> class so every reveal shows with JS disabled), <strong>LodgingBusiness + two VacationRental JSON-LD entities in an @graph</strong> (PostalAddress with the real PO Box + geo + telephone + email + foundingDate 1907 + petsAllowed + per-house bedrooms / bathrooms / occupancy / amenityFeature + sameAs), full OG / Twitter / geo / ICBM meta, canonical, favicon. QA&rsquo;d via Puppeteer at <strong>1440 + 390</strong> &mdash; <strong>0px horizontal overflow at both widths</strong> (two real overflow bugs were found and fixed: images&rsquo; intrinsic widths were flooring grid tracks via <code>min-width:auto</code>, and a <code>flex:none</code> was defeating the distance rule&rsquo;s wrapping), exactly one h1, all 16 reveals fire, 14 images all with alt (12 lazy), 0 inline style attributes, 0 &lt;style&gt; blocks, 0 onclick handlers, the rail shows at 1440 and is correctly replaced by the top bar + burger at 390, the drawer opens / Escape-closes / scroll-locks, the booking bar reveals and its date default lands on a <strong>Sunday (2026-08-09)</strong>, 0 JS errors. <strong>Images (rev. 2 &mdash; Steve supplied the real files):</strong> the first pass had to assign photo slots from <em>filenames</em>, because the sandbox is firewalled from weebly.com. Steve then uploaded 17 of them, and seeing them changed several assignments: <code>macgregor-sullivan</code> turned out to be <strong>the wrap-around porch, not an exterior</strong>, and <code>beach-sullivan-macgregor</code> is a <strong>superb aerial of both houses on the point</strong> with the Salt Pond, Crescent Beach and the ocean in one frame. <strong>15 photographs are now self-hosted in <code>/img/</code></strong> (EXIF-transposed, LANCZOS-resized, progressive JPEG q84, ~1.3 MB total), and <strong>every plate now sits in a frame matching its orientation</strong>: the portrait porch shot carries the hero&rsquo;s tall left plate, the landscape front elevation carries the MacGregor card lead, the sunset exterior is the small inset straddling the seam, and <strong>all three parallax bands are self-hosted</strong> &mdash; the aerial of the point under &ldquo;Graceful porches, commanding views&rdquo; and the Crescent Beach aerial under the beach band. The build also gained <strong>real MacGregor interiors</strong> (living room, kitchen, bath), which the first pass had none of, so each house now carries its own interior rather than sharing a set. Only <strong>four slots still hotlink</strong> the client&rsquo;s own server (the two Beach House exteriors, the higher-res MacGregor <code>_orig</code>, and their renovated kitchen), each with <code>referrerpolicy=&quot;no-referrer&quot;</code> and, where a local copy exists, a <code>data-fallback</code> attribute wired to an <code>error</code> listener in <code>main.js</code>; the CTA band uses a two-layer CSS <code>background-image</code> so the self-hosted aerial paints underneath if the hotlink fails. <code>/img/README.md</code> documents every slot. <strong>Rev. 3 &mdash; the drone set.</strong> Steve then supplied four <strong>4000&times;3000 golden-hour DJI frames</strong> of The MacGregor, which replaced every low-resolution aerial the build had been leaning on: <code>DJI_0153</code> cropped 4:5 is now the <strong>hero&rsquo;s tall left plate</strong> (the whole house, porch and Mansard roof, with the New Harbor mooring field stacked above it), <code>DJI_0156</code> is the <strong>MacGregor card lead</strong>, <code>DJI_0120</code> (sunset over the Great Salt Pond, the town and the open ocean) carries the <strong>&ldquo;Graceful porches, commanding views&rdquo; band</strong>, and <code>DJI_0148</code> (the house with the harbour thick with sailboats) carries the <strong>final CTA band</strong>. The porch shot moved down into the gallery. <strong>19 of 21 photographs are now self-hosted</strong> (~3.3 MB) and <strong>only three hotlinks remain, all of them Beach House images</strong>. og:image, twitter:image and both JSON-LD images now point at self-hosted files rather than Weebly. <strong>Rev. 4 &mdash; client direction (Steve, Aug 5): &ldquo;big beautiful hero photo/photos when you first come to the site&hellip; and red is NOT a good color for this &mdash; use yellow and green like the building and sunset colors.&rdquo;</strong> Two changes, both overriding earlier decisions. <strong>(1) The hero was rebuilt from the rolled asymmetric collage into a full-bleed photographic hero</strong> &mdash; a 78&ndash;88vh stage running a <strong>crossfade slider of three 4K drone frames</strong> (the house over the mooring field / the harbour thick with sailboats / sunset over the Salt Pond) with a gold dot picker, 6.5s autoplay that stops the moment a visitor picks a frame and never starts under <code>prefers-reduced-motion</code> or on a hidden tab. <strong>The photographs still run completely clean</strong> &mdash; no scrims, no gradients &mdash; with the copy in a solid pine panel, the same device every band already used. The four-cell fact row moved out from under the collage onto a paper band below the photograph. The fourth drone frame moved to the &ldquo;commanding views&rdquo; band, which was soft before. <strong>(2) The palette was re-sampled from the property&rsquo;s own photographs and the beach-rose accent removed entirely</strong> &mdash; no red anywhere on the page. The new system: <strong>dark scrub green #1E2A1C / #14200F</strong> (panels, dark bands, footer, booking bar) + <strong>moss #3F5A32</strong> (eyebrows, links, hairlines, the <em>em</em> accents on light grounds) + <strong>house yellow / sunset gold #D6A32E</strong> (every CTA, set with dark ink text rather than white) + <strong>pale gold #EDCD84</strong> (the <em>em</em> accents on dark grounds) + sage #77836D + chalk #F6F4EC + sand + a green-tinted mist. The gold is the actual clapboard and porch-baluster yellow of The MacGregor and the gold of the Salt Pond at sunset; the greens are the scrub and field of the bluff. <strong>Rev. 5 &mdash; nav (Steve): &ldquo;let&rsquo;s do a standard navigation and not a side one.&rdquo;</strong> The rolled side rail was replaced with a conventional <strong>top bar</strong>: a solid chalk sticky header carrying the wordmark left, five centred links with a gold underline that wipes in on hover, and a tap-to-call circle plus the gold Reserve button right, gaining a shadow on scroll. Below 1020px it collapses to wordmark + phone circle + Reserve + burger, with the links in the existing full-screen pine drawer &mdash; phone and Reserve stay visible at 390px. The <code>body</code> left padding, the booking bar&rsquo;s left offset and the vertical writing-mode rules all came out with it. <strong>Caveats</strong>: resolution is now <strong>resolved for The MacGregor</strong> (the drone set is 4000&times;3000 and carries every large slot) but <strong>not for The Beach House</strong>, which after three photo drops still has <strong>no local file at all</strong> &mdash; both the hero&rsquo;s right plate and its card lead hotlink Weebly. The MacGregor has four superb aerials and the Beach House none, which leaves the two halves of the diptych visibly unequal. The interiors top out at 1067px and the Crescent Beach aerial at 800px, so the beach band is still upscaled about 1.8&times; at 1440px; nothing is upscaled in the markup. The property has <strong>no logo</strong> (the live Weebly site uses a plain text title), so the rail and footer use a Yeseva wordmark; drop in a real mark to swap, and re-sample the palette from it. <strong>Their own pages disagree on several published figures</strong> and this build resolves each explicitly: rates use the reserve page&rsquo;s section headed &ldquo;for the 2026/27 Seasons&rdquo; ($7,500 / $12,500) rather than the homepage&rsquo;s $10,500 / $9,500 specials or the older &ldquo;2025 rates&rdquo; table; sleeps-12 is used for the Beach House (the same page says 11 in one line and 12 in two others); MacGregor October is shown as &ldquo;from $3,000&rdquo; where the reserve page says $4,000 and the homepage says $3,000. <strong>All of these need confirming with the owner before client-facing use.</strong> The magazine screenshots on their gallery page were deliberately not reproduced &mdash; the <em>Coastal Living</em> / <em>Elle</em> credential is carried in copy instead. The email address was recovered by decoding their Cloudflare-obfuscated mailto. Current-availability lines (specific 2027 weeks) were deliberately generalised so the page does not go stale. <strong>Two real layout bugs were found and fixed in the rev. 2 pass</strong>: the UA default <code>figure { margin: 1em 40px }</code> was never reset, so every photo plate was inset 40px and rendering 80px narrower than its grid track; and images&rsquo; intrinsic widths were flooring grid tracks via <code>min-width:auto</code>. <strong>Biggest upgrade remaining</strong>: <strong>a drone pass over The Beach House</strong> &mdash; it is now the only thing holding the page back &mdash; plus a high-resolution Crescent Beach frame and one shot from the widow&rsquo;s walk (which the copy sells and no photo shows). the property&rsquo;s own beach photograph running <strong>completely clean</strong> with a slow 30s drift &mdash; per Steve, <strong>no shade on any photo</strong>: this build carries zero scrims, gradients or overlays on any image, so every photograph shows at full value and the copy sits in solid claret panels instead, a tracked eyebrow, an H1 of their own headline (&ldquo;This is Traverse City <em>the way you like it.</em>&rdquo;), the real lede, dual CTA, and a chip row of the real specs (120 guest rooms / private beach / indoor pool &amp; whirlpool / complimentary breakfast / shuttle downtown), closed by the awning stripe. <strong>Sections (rolled &mdash; leads with the two-shores claim rather than an about; the beach gets its own full-bleed band before the rooms; the peninsula map sits before the gallery; meetings and guest scores come late)</strong>: <strong>Beach on one side, downtown on the other</strong> (their own best-of-both-worlds copy + a framed bayfront-balcony plate + a four-cell stat row: 120 rooms / 5 rate tiers by view / 4.4 Tripadvisor / #3 of 44) &rarr; a full-bleed <strong>beach band</strong> (clean photo, panelled copy) (&ldquo;The bay is the front yard.&rdquo; + the chairs-and-towels and Aussie Watersports notes) &rarr; <strong>Five ways to look at the bay</strong> (the five real tiers as a symmetric grid, the top two tiers widened to half-rows, each with a view badge, real bed configuration, real description and the view meter) &rarr; <strong>Amenities</strong> (12 real items in a hairline-separated 3&times;4 grid with hand-built inline-SVG icons) &rarr; <strong>Between two peninsulas</strong> (a real z=9 Google map of the bay + Old Mission / Leelanau / downtown, with a framed wine-country plate) &rarr; a 3&times;3 <strong>gallery</strong> with the aerial as a 2&times;2 feature tile &rarr; <strong>Meetings</strong> (the real capacity table, semantic <code>&lt;table&gt;</code> with row headers and a caption) &rarr; a claret <strong>guest scores</strong> band (the real 4.4 / 1,148 / #3 of 44 plus three recurring themes <em>summarised in our own words</em> with an explicit not-verbatim disclosure) &rarr; <strong>Visit</strong> (real address, both real phone numbers, fax, Get Directions, and the real policies) &rarr; an unshaded photographic <strong>final CTA</strong> (copy in a centred claret panel) (&ldquo;Come stay <em>on the bay.</em>&rdquo;) &rarr; a claret-ink footer (Stay / Explore / Get-in-touch columns wired to the real live Specials and Links pages, real Facebook, dynamic-year copyright, <strong>&ldquo;Concept by Bofill Technologies&rdquo;</strong> credit). <strong>Booking bar (the fixed mechanic, per the brief)</strong>: a slim claret bar with the awning stripe as its top rule, revealing once the hero scrolls out &mdash; a Bodoni &ldquo;Book direct&rdquo; label + Check-in / Check-out native date inputs (today / tomorrow defaults, checkout min auto-follows check-in) + an Adults select, collapsing to two date fields, a call circle and a full-width Check Availability on phones. <strong>It passes real dates</strong>: the property&rsquo;s own Book Now link exposed the engine&rsquo;s format, so the bar builds <code>arrival=MM-DD-YYYY&amp;departure=MM-DD-YYYY&amp;people1=N&amp;target=availability</code> into the live <strong>iRM</strong> engine (<code>reservations.bayshore-resort.com/irmng/#/search</code>) &mdash; a rare case where the dates actually carry through rather than sitting behind a marked hook. <strong>Motion (rolled: staggered reveal)</strong> &mdash; IntersectionObserver reveals released in 90ms batches, plus one slow hero drift; no parallax, no ticker, no fade-only. <strong>Built strictly to the Web Architect SOP</strong>: flat <code>index.html</code> at root + <code>/css/style.css</code> + <code>/js/main.js</code> + <code>/img/</code>, <strong>zero inline <code>style=""</code>, zero &lt;style&gt; blocks, zero executable inline &lt;script&gt; (JSON-LD + external src defer only), zero onclick handlers, zero JS <code>.style</code> assignments</strong> (all state via class toggles), BEM throughout, CSS custom properties at <code>:root</code>, mobile-first (560/768/1024/1280 breakpoints), vanilla ES6+ IIFE, WordPress-canonical nav classes (<code>.nav-primary</code>, <code>ul.menu.genesis-nav-menu.menu-primary</code>, <code>.menu-item</code>). Semantic HTML5, WCAG 2.1 (skip link, focus-visible, exactly one h1, alt on every image, explicit width/height, lazy below the fold, aria-labelled meters, titled map iframes, a captioned data table with row headers), <strong>Resort JSON-LD</strong> (PostalAddress + geo + telephone + faxNumber + numberOfRooms 120 + petsAllowed false + aggregateRating 4.4/1148 + 10 amenityFeature entries + sameAs), full OG / Twitter / geo / ICBM meta, canonical, favicon, <code>prefers-reduced-motion</code> honored. QA&rsquo;d via Puppeteer at <strong>1440 + 390</strong> &mdash; <strong>0px horizontal overflow at both widths</strong>, exactly one h1, all 22 reveals fire, 17 images all with alt (15 lazy), 0 inline style attributes, 0 &lt;style&gt; blocks, the drawer opens / Escape-closes / scroll-locks, the booking bar reveals with correct date defaults, 0 JS errors. <strong>Images</strong>: <strong>10 real property photos self-hosted in <code>/img/</code></strong> (Pillow-optimized, EXIF-transposed, progressive JPEG q84) &mdash; the four room interiors that map one-to-one onto the real tiers (standard / east side / bayfront / spa), the bayfront-balcony plate, the aerial of the resort with its beach and dock, the white-umbrella beach, the beach chairs, the balcony-over-the-beach frame, and the wine-country plate &mdash; <strong>plus 5 hotlinked from the client&rsquo;s own server</strong> with <code>referrerpolicy="no-referrer"</code> and JS fallbacks to local equivalents (the hero beach shot, the suite, the indoor pool, the lobby stairwell, a second beach frame) and the real logo. <strong>Caveats</strong>: the sandbox is firewalled from bayshore-resort.com, so the hotlinked slots and the logo render blank in build QA (they load fine live / on GitHub Pages, and every one has a working fallback) and <strong>the logo could not be opened to eyedrop its exact brand colours</strong> &mdash; the header is therefore deliberately light chalk so the real logo reads on it, and the claret/cherry/bay palette should be locked against the real file before client-facing use. <strong>Three of the attached photos were deliberately dropped</strong>: a tall balconied building, a long low shoreline building, and a wood-beamed indoor pool, none of which match the aerial that is unmistakably Bayshore (four storeys, long private beach, rows of white loungers, dock) &mdash; rather than risk showing a competitor&rsquo;s property they were pulled and the client&rsquo;s own pool / lobby files hotlinked instead. The winery frame is real regional context and is captioned as wine country beside the peninsula map, not presented as the resort. <strong>Biggest upgrade available</strong>: every attached beach and exterior photo arrived at roughly 357&times;201 &mdash; only the five room interiors came through at 1200px &mdash; so the hero currently leans on the client&rsquo;s own hosted file with an upscaled aerial as its fallback. Full-resolution versions of that aerial and the beach set, plus one confirmed suite interior, would transform the page. Guest notes are <strong>paraphrased, not quoted</strong>, and labelled as such. Rates were deliberately omitted (the live site publishes none). Homepage-only delivery. | Bodoni Moda + Epilogue | 2026-08-03 |
| 102 | Harbourside III | Hilton Head Island, SC (<strong>9 Harbourside Lane</strong> &middot; villas directly above the shops &amp; restaurants of <strong>Shelter Cove Harbour</strong> &middot; a Mediterranean-village harbour in the centre of the island &middot; <strong>39 quarter-share villas</strong>, one to three bedrooms &middot; on-site management &middot; 843-785-4000 / 800-542-5535) | [harboursideiii.com](https://harboursideiii.com/) | [View &rarr;](https://bofilltech.github.io/concepts/harbourside-iii/) | <strong>Off-centre framed hero + CENTRED SPLIT-LOGO nav + broken-asymmetric grid + subtle-fade motion.</strong> Homepage-only redesign concept. use the logo and pictures from the website or from [the Tripadvisor listing].&rdquo; Real content pulled live from harboursideiii.com (home / accommodations / about-us / villas) plus the property&rsquo;s own Tripadvisor listing: the real positioning in their own words (<strong>&ldquo;the epicenter of Hilton Head&rdquo;</strong>), the real in-villa spec (<strong>every villa furnished alike</strong> &mdash; king master, full kitchen, dining area, living room with a queen sleeper sofa, laundry machines, private balcony; 2BR adds a twin guest room, 3BR adds twins + a queen), the real amenity set (centre of the island, one mile to twelve miles of beach, gated secure parking, on-site pool &amp; spa, barbecue area, free WiFi, luxury furnishings, washer &amp; dryer in every villa), the real <strong>three miles of private Palmetto Dunes beach</strong> + the seasonal free <strong>Dunes Buggy shuttle</strong>, the real Harbourfest calendar (Music &amp; Taste on the Harbour, Shannon Tanner&rsquo;s family show Mon&ndash;Fri, his full band Thursdays, Shan Tan and the Oyster Reefers&rsquo; Parrot Palooza, <strong>Tuesday-night fireworks visible from select balconies</strong>, the fall festival run, and the holiday boat lights), the real quarter-share ownership model (four separately deeded owners per villa, no joint liability, thirteen weeks a year, quarters rotating one week annually), the real booking constraint (<strong>Sunday-to-Sunday weeks booked at least 14 days out</strong> &mdash; shorter or sooner, call the office) and the real Tripadvisor scores (<strong>4.7 / 75 reviews, Value 5.0</strong>, Location 4.9, Rooms 4.9, Sleep 4.9, Service 4.7, Cleanliness 4.6). <strong>SIGNATURE DEVICE: THE VIEW PICKER.</strong> Their own copy says villas are furnished alike so you always know what to expect &mdash; which means the only real decision a guest makes is <em>what they look at</em>. So the rooms section is rebuilt as an accessible tablist over the property&rsquo;s three actual view categories, with the real per-category villa counts derived from their own unit lists: <strong>Direct harbour 16 of 39</strong> (2 one-bed + 12 two-bed + 2 three-bed) / <strong>Entertainment pavilion 12</strong> (9 two-bed + 3 three-bed) / <strong>The market 11</strong> (1 one-bed + 10 two-bed) &mdash; every figure reconciled against the 39-villa total and their published unit numbers. Backed by three oversized brass numerals for the real layout counts (<strong>3 / 31 / 5</strong>). The headline claim: &ldquo;Every villa is furnished alike. <em>The view is the decision.</em>&rdquo; <strong>Second device: the KING NEPTUNE SUNDIAL</strong> &mdash; a hand-built inline-SVG sundial eyebrow glyph, content-true because the larger-than-life King Neptune sundial stands right outside the building and is the landmark in their own driving directions; it also anchors <strong>&ldquo;The harbour year&rdquo;</strong>, the season section rendered as a four-chapter ordered timeline (March&rarr;May / mid-June&rarr;mid-Aug / mid-Aug&rarr;Oct / the holidays) &mdash; numbering earned because it is a real calendar, not decoration. <strong>Palette sampled from the property&rsquo;s own world, not a generic coastal navy</strong>: <strong>harbour petrol #123C46 / #0B272E</strong> (the basin at dusk &mdash; dark bands, footer, booking bar; a green-blue, deliberately not near-black) + <strong>sundial brass #C4902F / #A5761F</strong> (the bronze Neptune sundial &mdash; every CTA, set with petrol text rather than low-contrast white) + <strong>shallow aqua #2E7F84</strong> (links, eyebrows, hairlines) + <strong>harbour mist #E4EBEA</strong> cool pale bands + <strong>paper #FCFBF9</strong> + oyster #DCD6C9 borders. Deliberately clears all three AI defaults &mdash; the ground is a cool mist/paper rather than warm cream, the accent is brass + aqua rather than terracotta, and the dark is a petrol teal rather than near-black. <strong>Type (both fresh to the ledger)</strong>: <strong>Syne</strong> (an idiosyncratic contemporary grotesque with unusually wide, architectural caps &mdash; hero, section titles, tracked eyebrows, the numerals, buttons) + <strong>Petrona</strong> (a warm variable serif carrying the body copy &mdash; a serif body being itself a departure from the grotesque bodies of the recent run). Phone collapses to a tappable circle and Book Now stays in the bar at 390px, with links in a petrol full-screen drawer (aria-expanded, Escape-close, scroll-lock, resize-close). <strong>HERO (rolled: off-centre framed)</strong> &mdash; not full-bleed: a cool mist field with the copy block left and the harbour photo held in a paper frame offset right and dropped below the band edge, stamped in brass; sundial eyebrow, an H1 of their own claim (&ldquo;Above the <em>epicenter</em> of Hilton Head.&rdquo;), the real lede, dual CTA, and a chip row of the real specs (39 villas / one, two &amp; three bedrooms / full kitchen &amp; laundry / private balcony / Sunday-to-Sunday weeks). <strong>Sections (rolled &mdash; leads with the villas and the view taxonomy, not an about; the harbour story carries the amenities narrative; a 6-tile broken-asymmetric <strong>gallery</strong> &rarr; <strong>Guest scores</strong> (the real 4.7 set out with all six sub-scores, headlined on Value 5.0, plus three guest notes <em>summarised in our own words</em> with an explicit not-verbatim disclosure) &rarr; body) + **Spline Sans Mono** (utility mono, earned in a business of SKUs and dimensions &mdash; eyebrows, buttons, labels). **Nav**: centered split-logo (links left / wordmark centre / phone + CTA right), solid chipboard bar w/ blur &rarr; Strictly navy + royal-blue + white &mdash; **no coral, no seafoam, no warm gold** (the first pass derived those from the photos rather than the brand; corrected per Steve); gold is reserved **only** for the review star glyphs (a universal rating convention). **Typography (both fresh to the tracker, deliberately avoiding the overused Fraunces / Cormorant / Playfair run)**: **Instrument Serif** (an elegant high-contrast display serif w/ italic accents &mdash; hero, section titles, room names, the welcome lede, the reviews score) + **Schibsted Grotesk** (a clean modern grotesque &mdash; nav, body, buttons, letterspaced eyebrows). **Signature device**: a hand-built inline-SVG **wave-flourish glyph** (echoing the double-wave motif in the property's real logo &mdash; the first pass had used an off-brand red beach umbrella) recurring as the eyebrow mark across every section (royal-blue on light grounds, white on photo/dark bands, light-blue on the navy booking bar) &mdash; and the same motif scaled up into **animated wave-flourish section dividers** (an elegant double-wave that *draws itself in* on scroll-reveal + a gentle infinite "breeze" sway, reduced-motion-safe) marking the editorial transitions. **Real logo**: the client's actual "Hartman's / briney breezes / BEACH RESORT" navy script logo (uploaded by Steve) is **embedded as base64** &mdash; the navy version in the nav, and a **white version generated from the same file** (Pillow alpha-recolor) for the navy footer &mdash; so the file is fully self-contained with no hotlinks or fallback wordmark. Book Now opens the real RMS Cloud IBE; its checkin/checkout query-param format isn't confirmed, so dates aren't yet passed (a marked hook in `js/main.js` is where they get wired in). Our customers do." → reframed, no fabricated reviews/names); **(11) clean editorial Visit block** (Saltie Girl location style) — big address, clickable phone, two real hours tables (in-season May–Sep / off-season Oct–Apr w/ weather caveat) + live Google Maps embed; **(12) full-bleed final CTA** ("Try us once. *You'll be back.*") + navy footer with the seal repeated, real Facebook/Instagram links, and the Bofill credit. **Typography overhaul**: dropped Bodoni Moda + Work Sans for **Bricolage Grotesque** (variable, characterful, very legible — handles the bold Mason's all-caps + the now-legible nav) + **Newsreader** (elegant editorial serif/italic for the Saltie Girl manifesto, split-heading top-words, and the "famously simple" moment). **Palette**: richer lobster coral `#d9402c` + coral-deep `#b8311f` + deep ink navy `#16242e` + navy-soft `#23394a` + warm cream `#f5ecdb` + butter `#fbf4e6` + ivory `#fffdf8` + sea-green teal `#2f7d72` (Saltie Girl accent) + gold `#cf9b3e` + sand — used as full color-block section grounds (Mason's), not white-with-cards. Sticky nav switches from transparent-over-hero (white) to a solid butter-cream fixed bar (navy text) past the hero; coral ORDER ONLINE + clickable phone always visible (mobile keeps Order Online out of the hamburger). Single-file concept (CSS + JS inline), mobile-first, IntersectionObserver scroll reveals, prefers-reduced-motion honored, QA'd at 1440 + 390 (no overflow, no JS errors). All 16 photos hotlinked from their own WordPress uploads with `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"` (render in a real browser; blank only in the sandbox, which blocks their domain — can re-host via `wp_upload_media` if any fail live). **Caveats**: roll descriptions kept light + brand-true since their site doesn't publish prep detail (get their real roll copy before client-facing use); homepage-only single-file delivery, not split to the Web Architect SOP (can refactor on request). | Bricolage Grotesque + Newsreader | May 30 (v2 redesign) |

Phone 516.660.8578. The descriptor she earns repeatedly across testimonials is "calm, organized, thorough, thoughtful" — so the concept frames her as quiet authority rather than guidance-counselor cheerful. **Built strictly to the Web Architect SOP from the project's CLAUDE_WEBSITE_STRUCTURE_GUIDE.md**: flat HTML at root, separate `/css/style.css` + `/js/main.js`, `/img/` folder with README documenting asset migration paths, zero inline `style=""`, zero `<style>` blocks, zero `<script>` blocks, zero `onclick` handlers, zero JS-driven `.style.prop` assignments (body scroll-lock + reveal animations done via class toggling against CSS rules), BEM throughout, CSS custom properties at `:root`, mobile-first responsive (720px tablet, 1024px desktop breakpoints), vanilla ES6+ in IIFE pattern. Semantic HTML5 (`<header> <main> <section> <article> <figure> <blockquote> <address>`), WCAG 2.1 (skip link, ARIA labels, focus-visible outlines, alt text, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected). **Palette**: deep navy ink `#1A2B3C` (Ivy collegiate trust) + warm brass `#B08D57` (achievement accent, like an old library brass plaque rather than gaudy gold) + brass-soft `#D4B98A` (light-mode brass for dark backgrounds) + paper cream `#F5EFE4` (warm sections) + bone `#FBF8F2` (off-white page) + charcoal `#2A2622` (body text) + slate `#6B6359` (muted text) + warm hairline `#D8CFC0`. **Typography**: **Fraunces** (variable display serif with optical-sizing axis dialed to 144 for headlines — characterful curves, modern editorial collegiate feel) + **Manrope** (geometric sans body, deliberately not Inter — modern but distinctive). **Sections**: dark-navy utility bar (Long Island NY tagline + Call Anne 516.660.8578 + Student Log-in), sticky main header with logo wordmark + WP-conventioned nav with hover dropdown for grade-level pages (Freshman/Sophomore/Junior/Senior) + brass "Schedule a Consultation" CTA, hero with full-bleed real `group1.jpg` photo + dual gradient overlay + Ken Burns 14s scale animation + staggered fadeUp on two-line headline ("Find your fit." + italic brass-soft "Find your future.") + Fraunces italic optical-144 styling + lede pulled from her real "35+ years" copy + dual CTA (brass Schedule + ghost Meet Anne) + vertical "Begin" scroll cue with animated drip line. **Trust ribbon** on dark navy with 4 brass-soft Fraunces stats divided by hairline rules (35+ Years / Harvard M.Ed. / Grades 9–12 / NACAC · IECA). **Meet Anne intro**: editorial split layout — left has her real `annTich.jpg` portrait inside a brass-frame offset (border + transform behind the image) topped with a brass-rule caption card on cream paper ("Anne Gould" Fraunces italic + "Founder & Independent College Advisor" eyebrow), right has eyebrow + "A calm, organized partner for one of life's biggest decisions." headline + lede + body paragraph paraphrased from her real Meet Anne copy + 3-row credentials list with italic-brass key letters (B.A. / M.Ed. / C.A.G.S. — Boston, Harvard, Lesley) bordered top + bottom + sky-blue-underlined link-arrow "Read Anne's full story →". **The Four Phases**: centered eyebrow + "Four phases. *One thoughtful journey.*" italic brass accent headline + lede, then a 4-card grid where each card carries an oversized italic brass Roman numeral (I / II / III / IV), a brass top-rule, and the real four-phase content (Self-Assessment / College Exploration / Application Assistance / Decisions & Next Steps) paraphrased from her own copy. **Desktop polish**: even-index cards translate down `var(--sp-6)` so the row reads as a staggered editorial rhythm rather than a generic four-up. Hover lifts each card with shadow. **Testimonials** on dark navy with oversized decorative Fraunces opening quote mark positioned top-right (28rem, opacity 0.5), 4 real testimonials (Roslyn NY parent → NYU Tisch, Woodmere NY parent → Emory, Cornell student, Barnard junior) trimmed to ~3 sentences each, each in a brass-left-rule quote block with author + location/school meta. **Associations**: 4-column grid of real association logos (IECA / NACAC / NYSACAC / NYSSCA) with grayscale-to-color hover transition + full association names beneath each (Independent Educational Consultants Association / National Association for College Admission Counseling / New York State Association for College Admission Counseling / New York State School Counselor Association). **Final CTA**: full-bleed real `greeting.jpg` photo with strong 120deg navy overlay + brass-soft eyebrow "Your journey starts here" + "Let's talk about your next chapter." Fraunces headline + lede + dual CTA (brass Schedule a Consultation + ghost-light "Or call 516.660.8578"). **4-column footer** on dark navy: brand col with logo + tagline + Fraunces-italic brass phone display + nav cols (Explore / By Grade / Get in Touch) + bottom base strip with copyright + "Concept by Bofill Technologies" credit. JS handles mobile nav toggle (aria-expanded, Escape close, link-tap close on mobile, resize reset on desktop), sticky header `is-scrolled` shadow state, footer year, and IntersectionObserver scroll reveals on `.phase / .quote / .meet__media / .meet__body / .creds__item` with stagger delays in CSS. **Asset migration note**: since the sandbox couldn't curl annegouldcollegeadvisor.com directly (not in allowlist) and the BofillTech WP MCP wasn't loaded in this session, the HTML references the client's existing CDN URLs at `annegouldcollegeadvisor.com/wp-content/uploads/` (her own assets on her own server — fine for a concept pitch). `/img/README.md` documents the migration table for WP conversion. Affordable Cape Cod beachfront vacation rental condos run by Tim Norton — 38 individually-owned units + free-standing cottages, heated freshwater pool, steps to Nantucket Sound, 5★ Tripadvisor, "since 1965." **v2 REDESIGN** after Steve flagged that v1 was too close to The Garlands (both used Fraunces + DM Sans + navy + coral). Complete palette + typography overhaul: **NO navy, NO coral, NO Fraunces, NO DM Sans**. **New palette**: postcard sky blue (#4FB3D9) + postcard red (#D62828) + sunshine yellow (#FFC857) + salmon pink (#F4A698) + aged paper cream (#FAF6E8) + deep sea ink (#1B3A4B). **Typography**: **Ultra** (extremely bold slab-serif, classic vintage travel-poster face — totally distinct from Fraunces' refined italic-led elegance) + **Familjen Grotesk** (geometric sans for labels/eyebrows/captions) + **Manrope** (body). **Hero is now a literal postcard**: bright sky-blue background with peeking sun in the corner, left side has "GREETINGS FROM" eyebrow + massive ULTRA "The Oceanside" + "Dennis Port · Cape Cod · Since 1965" in red eyebrow + chunky double-bordered red Check Availability button with hard offset shadow, right side has the aerial photo set inside a white postcard frame (rotated -1.5°, 2px black border, hard shadow + yellow offset shadow, rotates to 0 on hover) with a "60 SUMMERS" red stamp graphic outline-bordered and rotated 8°, hand-typeset postcard caption "The Oceanside · Dennis Port / Old Wharf Road · Cape Cod, Massachusetts" along the bottom. Brand mark: yellow circle with red wave + sun (not just text). **Facts strip is now a bright yellow band** (not dark navy) with red Ultra numbers (60/38/★5.0/$165) bordered top and bottom in ink. **Welcome section**: photo in another small postcard frame rotated 1° with caption "The patio — cocktail hour" and the real Tripadvisor pull quote in a salmon-pink card with thick black border and hard shadow. **Pool & Sound feature**: light sky-blue band with photo in postcard frame, amenity list with chunky red circle-checkmarks. **Stay cards**: chunky 2px-bordered cards with red tag pills (OCEAN VIEW / POOLSIDE / BALCONY / STANDALONE), Ultra serif room names, dashed-line meta dividers, hover lifts with hard offset shadow. **Cape Cod section** on sky-blue: six cream postcard cards with yellow-circled bordered icons that flip to red on hover, Ultra display names. **Gallery is now a polaroid stack** — each photo in white postcard frame with handwritten-style label underneath ("PICTURE WINDOW" / "COZY BEDROOM" / "THE DINING NOOK" / "SITTING ROOM" / "THE BALCONY"), each card slightly rotated different angles, hover rotates to 0 + lifts. **CTA banner is now bright red** (not dark navy) with white headline + yellow "Cape Cod Summer?" emphasis + yellow chunky Call button with black shadow + white-outlined Email Tim button. **Location map** sits in a bordered postcard frame with hard shadow. **Bottom booking bar is now red** (not navy) with yellow labels + yellow Book button with hard black shadow. All Web Architect SOP standards retained — flat HTML, /css /js /img, zero inline styles/scripts/onclick, BEM, WCAG 2.1, mobile-first, vanilla ES6+. | Ultra + Familjen Grotesk + Manrope | May 17 (v2) |

Drop-in ready for WordPress theme conversion. **Palette**: deep pine (#1F2D24) + cream (#F4EDE0) + burnt orange (#C46A1E) + sun-aged gold (#D9A55C) + warm bark. **Typography**: Big Shoulders Display 800 uppercase for display (rugged trade-magazine feel), Spectral italic for em-accents in rust, JetBrains Mono for coordinates / labels. **Sections**: dark utility bar (Free estimates · 24/7 emergency · Practiced Professionals Since 1993 · phone CTA), sticky main nav with hamburger collapse + Get a Free Estimate CTA, hero with the Jim-Gaun-with-big-log photo + rotating "33 YEARS ON THE RIM" badge in rust + coordinate stamp, animated marquee of services on dark pine band, 5-icon trust strip with custom SVG icons (Yosemite-Trained Heritage / 24/7 Emergency / Free Written Estimates / Fully Insured / Firewise Property Mitigation), **five service cards** with Roman numerals I-V (Hazardous Removal / Pruning / Thinning & Firewise / Lot Clearing / **Insurance Compliance** with "Now offering" flag — Jordan specifically asked to emphasize this emerging carrier-driven service line, V card sits on cream-warm to visually mark it), "Also on the truck" tag-pill row (Stump Grinding / Crane Removal / Man-Lift Work / Advanced Rigging / Emergency Response / Hauling & Cleanup), dark pine **Story section** rewritten with the real history Jordan shared (El Capitan, NPS film, Jordan-at-16, 20 years, Jim passed 2021, 30-year right-hand man, young crew) + Jordan-voiced pull quote, **NEW Trusted By plate** on cream-warm — editorial credits-page register with mono numbers + Big Shoulders Display client names + role tags (01 National Park Service · 02 City of Flagstaff · 03 Arizona DOT · 04 Coconino County · 05 Flagstaff Unified Schools · 06 Golf Courses & Estates) + footnote "Licensed with the State of Arizona · Workers' Compensation · General Liability", **Service Area** with stylized Northern AZ SVG map (Coconino region outlined in rust dashes, gold compass rose, animated pulsing star on Flagstaff HQ, pine tree silhouettes), 4-card "Why Neighbors Keep Calling Us Back" grid (all "certified" language stripped → "practiced arborists" + "professional climbing systems"), full-bleed dark pine **Final CTA** with massive "(928) 606-4371" phone button + 3-col contact grid, comprehensive footer with dynamic year + Concept by Bofill Technologies credit. Sticky mobile call-bar always visible. JS handles mobile nav toggle (aria-expanded), Escape key close, smooth scroll with nav offset, IntersectionObserver reveal-on-scroll for service/why/area/trust/clients rows with stagger, respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. Logo + Jim photo + family photo all uploaded to bofilltech.com. **Inner pages added (May 16)**: `services.html` (5-block deep dive — Hazardous Removal, Pruning, Thinning & Firewise, Lot Clearing, Stump Grinding — each with two-pane "what we do / how we approach it" layout, plus a dark-pine Emergency Response block with rust CTA); `about.html` (long-form 3-chapter story page: Jim's El Cap chapter on dark pine / Jordan's chapter on cream-warm / today; 3-card crew section [Jordan / 30-year right-hand man / younger crew]; 4-card philosophy grid on dark pine; "What's on the truck" fleet list referencing 18" chippers, loaders with grapples, man lifts); `service-area.html` (6-row coverage list — Flagstaff HQ rust-tinted, Williams, Sedona & Oak Creek, Munds Park, Bellemont, wider Coconino — each row has coords, descriptive blurb, notes-as-chip-pills for response time / service mix / common work type; stylized Northern AZ SVG map below); `insurance-compliance.html` (dedicated lead-gen landing page: dark pine hero with "Now Offering" rust flag + "Schedule an Assessment" CTA; "Why this matters" rows — non-renewal letters / defensible-space teeth / documentation matters / fly-by-night operators won't pass; "Who needs this" checked list; 4-step process grid Assess→Document→Execute→Renew); `contact.html` (3-card contact grid Phone/Email/Yard with rust icons; 24/7 emergency callout band with pulsing rust dot; hours-and-location 2-col with Mon–Sat 7am–6pm and Bader Rd address; full estimate request form on dark pine with required-field markers — Your Name, Phone, Email, Property Address, What You Need [select], How Urgent [select], Tell Us About The Tree [textarea] — plus "Send Request" rust CTA and legal footnote). All 6 pages share the same nav with rust active-state underline on current page. Flyout menu is a `<aside>` that slides in from the left with the navy wordmark + close button at top, big Cormorant Garamond serif nav links (Rooms & Suites · Restaurant 1879 · Weddings & Events · Gallery · Block Island · Contact · Rose Farm Inn ↗), address block + clickable phone in the footer; a 50% black scrim covers the page behind it. Below the hero: centered intro headline + body, full-width 6-image horizontal scroll-snap carousel with uppercase caption labels (THE INN — OLD HARBOR / THE ENTRY HALL / etc.), centered italic pull quote, 2-col Rooms feature (tag → italic title → body → outlined "Find Your Room" CTA → ONE portrait image with overlaid italic caption instead of 4 cards), "What people are saying" 3-column press testimonials band on `--paper-2` (Fodor's / Wine Spectator / OpenTable Diners' Choice), centered Restaurant 1879 dining section (eyebrow → italic name → wide food banner → body → "View the Menus" underlined link), full-width dark navy Weddings & Events band with eyebrow + giant headline + body + 3-image grid + outlined "Plan Your Event" CTA, italic "The veranda is waiting." closing with email newsletter signup, 4-column dark midnight footer (brand + The Inn + Gather + Visit) with consolidated nav (Manor folded into Rooms & Suites, Weddings + Events combined, Rose Farm Inn external link with ↗). Same palette and Cormorant Garamond + Manrope typography as v0 for brand consistency across both concepts. **v1 inner pages (NEW, Jun 06)**: Full multi-page site built out — 7 inner pages all in matching Wythe layout language with shared 4-corner hero pattern (Menu / Book / italic page slogan / address) and consistent flyout nav. Each page uses real atlanticinn.com content paraphrased into Wythe-style editorial structure. **(1) /rooms-suites/** — 7 room cards in 2-col grid (Island View Queen, Ocean View King, Cozy Room, Cozy Family Suite, Studio Suite, Veranda Suite, Penthouse Suite) with type/sleeps brass tags, italic-accent room names, descriptions paraphrased from atlanticinn.com, "SEE DETAILS" underlines + dark "Beyond the Inn" Manor cross-promo band. **(2) /restaurant-1879/** — hero with food bg, "An unparalleled dining experience" intro + Reserve a Table CTA, 4-image carousel, Chef Francis Cavanagh reverse-feature, paper-2 accolades band (Bon Appétit · Wine Spectator · Travel + Leisure · Gourmet · Providence Journal · Yankee · Rhode Island Magazine), hours grid (Dinner Sun-Thu 5-9 / Fri-Sat 5-10 / Lunch Sat-Sun 11-3 / OpenTable + 401.466.5883), pull quote. **(3) /weddings-events/** — "Memories that evoke a lifetime" intro, "A special day in a special place" wedding feature, paper-2 capacity stats band (6ac · 23 rooms · 2-200 guests · 1 property all yours), "Discreet, attentive, impeccable" meetings feature, 4-image events carousel, dark Inquire band linking to /weddings/start-planning/. **(4) /the-manor/** — hero with manor exterior, "Timeless New England charm. Upscale coastal living" intro, paper-2 specs band (4 bedrooms · 8 beds · 4.5 baths · 4 min walk to beach), "A chef's kitchen, framed by ocean views" feature, "Cozy yet refined" reverse-feature, pull quote, dark "Make The Manor yours" CTA → /contact/. **(5) /gallery/** — 9-photo asymmetric editorial grid with uppercase caption overlays (The Inn at Old Harbor / A Garden View Queen / The Entry Hall / The Sitting Room / Restaurant 1879 / An Ocean View King / A Garden Room / A Suite / The Manor), pull quote, dark "See it for yourself" Book With Us CTA. **(6) /block-island/** — "An island unto itself" intro, "Things To Do" 2-col grid with 8 destinations + distance meta + descriptions (Crescent Beach 1.7mi · Mohegan Bluffs 3mi · Old Harbor 0.5mi · Great Salt Pond 2mi · North Lighthouse 5mi · Clayhead Trail 3.5mi · Ballard's Beach 0.8mi · Block Island Historical Society Museum 1mi), dark "Three ferries, a small plane, and a quiet welcome" getting-here band, pull quote. **(7) /contact/** — hero with shells-art entry hall photo, "However you'd like to reach us" intro, 2-col layout: left = contact info dl (Phone 401.466.5884 / Restaurant 401.466.5883 / Email atlanticinn@biri.com / 359 High St / Front Desk Hours / Instagram + Facebook), right = "Send a note" form (Name / Email / Phone optional / How can we help? textarea / Send button), dark "Or just book a stay" Book With Us CTA. **Homepage updated**: flyout nav now expanded from 5 destinations to 7 (added The Manor + Block Island as standalone tabs), all internal links from atlanticinn.com URLs swapped to internal paths (rooms-suites/ · restaurant-1879/ · weddings-events/ · the-manor/ · gallery/ · block-island/ · contact/), footer columns updated to match. **Shared system**: each page is a single self-contained HTML file with embedded base64 image assets (~1-2.5MB per page); shared CSS (~12KB) duplicated per page rather than externalized to keep deployment simple on GitHub Pages; consistent flyout JS with Escape/click-scrim/click-link close, body scroll lock when open; the active page is highlighted in italic rose in the flyout nav. Photography limited to v0's 11 unique assets — reused strategically across pages (food in restaurant + carousels; rooms images in rooms grid + dining carousels; exterior in weddings + block-island heroes; entry hall in contact hero + carousels; manor exterior on Manor page hero).
